  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Catherine, leader of Russia (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Russia
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Cossack, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Licorne, Replacing Field Gun (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Ostrog, Replacing Bastion Fort (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Building: Pogost, Replacing Customs House (Requires technology: Guilds)
        - Civilization Ability: All Strategic Resource deposits provide double their normal quantity. Receive 20 Science when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Culture cost of acquiring new tiles reduced by 33%.
      - Leader: Catherine
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

Nuclear Fission unlocks Atomic Bombs - essential for breaking Edinburgh and London defenses efficiently. With Delhi falling this turn, atomic weapons will accelerate capture of remaining capitals (Edinburgh, London, Washington) for domination victory.         You, Catherine (leader of Russia, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 424.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Catherine, leader of Russia (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Russia
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Cossack, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Licorne, Replacing Field Gun (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Ostrog, Replacing Bastion Fort (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Building: Pogost, Replacing Customs House (Requires technology: Guilds)
        - Civilization Ability: All Strategic Resource deposits provide double their normal quantity. Receive 20 Science when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Culture cost of acquiring new tiles reduced by 33%.
      - Leader: Catherine
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the world (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Delhi's Capture**
            
            Delhi has fallen, as anticipated, marking a significant shift. My focus is now on evaluating the immediate aftermath and formulating our next strategic moves with this new reality in mind.
            
            **Assessing Ownership Changes**
            
            The situation regarding Rome is indeed complex. It appears I was mistaken about its recent ownership history. My current assessment confirms Russia now holds Moscow and Delhi, but Rome is actually still under our control, not Russia's. This significantly alters our capital count.
            
            **Discovering New Capitals**
            
            I've realized Thebes, an original capital, is occupied by India. Capturing it would add to our capital count. I'm also re-evaluating our current wars, noticing several city-states, allied with India, are at war with us.
            
            **Clarifying Diplomatic Alliances**
            
            I've been reviewing diplomatic stances and discovered a misunderstanding regarding our engagement with city-states allied with India. It seems Djenne, previously thought to be allied with India due to influence, is indeed a major ally. Further, I've clarified that we are not at war with the Celts, but rather bound by a peace treaty preventing immediate hostilities.
            
            **Analyzing Defensive Pacts**
            
            I've uncovered a critical detail: America has a defensive pact with the Celts. This means a war with America would likely drag the Celts into conflict with us, complicating my earlier assessment of our relationship with them. I need to understand the implications of this before proceeding.
            
            **Evaluating Defensive Pacts**
            
            I'm focusing on the intricacies of defensive pacts and peace treaties. Specifically, I'm trying to determine how a defensive pact between America and the Celts interacts with our existing peace treaty with the Celts, and whether declaring war on America would force us into conflict with them, potentially bypassing our current peace.
            
            **Identifying New Capitals**
            
            My focus has shifted to identifying newly available capitals. I've confirmed Pataliputra as India's current capital and Thebes, formerly Egypt's, as another potential acquisition. Capturing both would significantly increase our capital count.
            
            **Recalculating Victory Conditions**
            
            I've had a crucial realization regarding the Domination victory. It's about controlling original capitals, not just any capitals. We currently hold Delhi, Rome, and Moscow, our original capitals. Thebes, India's original capital, is now held by India and is a prime target.
            
            **Targeting Original Capitals**
            
            I'm now laser-focused on securing all original capitals. Delhi, Rome, and Moscow are ours. Thebes, currently under Indian control, is now my primary objective. London, Edinburgh, Washington, and Karakorum represent the remaining targets for our Domination victory push.
            
            **Formulating Capture Strategies**
            
            My attention is now directed towards acquiring the remaining original capitals. Thebes is held by India, London by England, Edinburgh by the Celts, Washington by America, and Karakorum by Mongolia. I'm strategizing how best to capture each of these crucial cities to achieve our Domination victory.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Obstacles**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our path to victory, specifically concerning diplomatic and military obstacles. With the original capitals identified, I'm now assessing which nations hold them and what alliances might complicate our capture efforts, particularly considering the defensive pact between America and the Celts.
            
            **Prioritizing Original Capital Acquisition**
            
            My focus is now squarely on acquiring the remaining original capitals required for Domination victory. I've identified Thebes, London, Edinburgh, Washington, and Karakorum as our next targets and am prioritizing them based on current geopolitical landscapes and potential conflicts.
            
            **Targeting Original Capitals**
            
            My immediate focus is on securing the remaining original capitals. I've calculated that capturing Thebes, London, Edinburgh, and Washington, alongside Karakorum, will grant us 100% of the original capitals. I am now assessing our relationship with Mongolia, our current enemies, and potential diplomatic or military ramifications of initiating new wars.
            
            **Analyzing American Proximity**
            
            My current focus is on the strategic positioning of American cities relative to our own, particularly near our newly captured Delhi and existing western territories like Agra and Varanasi. This proximity raises concerns about opening a third front if we declare war on America while heavily engaged with India.
            
            **Assessing Combined Threat**
            
            My focus is on the immediate tactical implications. America's eastern cities are deeply interwoven with India's, meaning any conflict with America now would immediately pit us against their forces in the current Indian theater. Our current military strength (8135) versus India (1909) and America (3788) suggests this could be a dangerous two-front scenario.
            
            **Analyzing Defensive Pacts**
            
            I'm now keenly aware that declaring war on America will also bring the Celts into conflict with us, due to their defensive pact. This complicates my previous assumption of a manageable two-front war, as it now potentially becomes three fronts, with the Celts' pacts with both America and England now relevant.
            
            **Analyzing Celtic Proximity**
            
            I'm now assessing our immediate proximity to Celtic cities, noting that Truro and Rome are particularly close. Their defensive strengths and unit compositions are concerning, especially with our current limited defenses in those zones. This requires a swift re-evaluation of our northern and eastern fronts.
            
            **Assessing Global War Readiness**
            
            I'm now confronting the potential for a massive global conflict if I initiate hostilities with America. This would pit us against India, England, and the Celts, due to their defensive pacts. However, Mongolia appears to be ideologically aligned and hostile towards these same nations, potentially offering a crucial ally in this widespread engagement.
            
            **Assessing Global War Readiness**
            
            I'm now confronting the potential for a massive global conflict if I initiate hostilities with America. This would pit us against India, England, and the Celts, due to their defensive pacts. However, Mongolia appears to be ideologically aligned and hostile towards these same nations, potentially offering a crucial ally in this widespread engagement.
            
            **Considering Atomic Bombs**
            
            I'm weighing the strategic advantage of delaying our engagement with America and the Celts until after we've potentially researched Atomic Theory. Discovering Uranium and developing Atomic Bombs could drastically alter the outcome of a global conflict, allowing for the complete obliteration of enemy cities. I need to assess if this delay is feasible given the upcoming World Congress vote and the potential for immediate threats.
            
            **Analyzing World Congress**
            
            I'm deeply concerned about the impending World Congress votes. The "Global Peace Accords" and "Sanction: Russia" proposals are detrimental to our aggressive expansion. With India, the Celts, and America likely voting together, their 47 delegates heavily outweigh our 21, making passage of these harmful resolutions almost certain without intervention.
            
            **Disrupting Delegate Counts**
            
            My current focus is on strategically disrupting India's delegate count within the World Congress. I'm assessing the feasibility of capturing Kathmandu, which is currently at war with us, as this would deny India a significant bloc of delegates. This approach bypasses the direct capture of India's core cities.
            
            **Disrupting India's Delegates**
            
            I've determined that eliminating India in the next eight turns by capturing their remaining cities is infeasible. Instead, I'm focusing on disrupting their delegate count to prevent detrimental World Congress votes, specifically targeting Kathmandu to deny them critical influence.
            
            **Disrupting India's Delegates**
            
            I've determined that eliminating India in the next eight turns by capturing their remaining cities is infeasible. Instead, I'm focusing on disrupting their delegate count to prevent detrimental World Congress votes, specifically targeting Kathmandu to deny them critical influence. My focus has shifted to disrupting India's delegate count to prevent detrimental World Congress votes by capturing Kathmandu, as eliminating India entirely is infeasible.
            
            **Capturing Djenne**
            
            I've identified an opportunity to swiftly capture Djenne. It's currently at war with us and strategically positioned between Wellington and Rome. With our existing military strength in the area, I anticipate securing it within 2-3 turns, which will significantly disrupt India's alliances.
            
            **Leveraging Gold Reserves**
            
            I'm now focusing on our immense gold reserves and per turn income. It seems I can leverage this substantial wealth to influence city-states and secure alliances, a diplomatic avenue I previously underestimated given my low Diplomacy flavor.
            
            **Acquiring Diplomatic Allies**
            
            I'm now leveraging our considerable gold reserves to secure city-state alliances and increase our delegate count for the World Congress. Flipping Vilnius from Celtic influence is a priority, as this denial of an ally and acquisition of delegates will prevent harmful resolutions like "Sanction: Russia" from passing.
            
            **Securing Broadcast Towers**
            
            I've discovered we possess at least five cities with Broadcast Towers: Truro, Rome, Delhi, Varanasi, and Agra. Since these are annexed and not puppets, they likely fulfill the quest's criteria. Completing this will grant a substantial influence boost, securing Vilnius's alliance and disrupting India's delegate count.
            
            **Securing Key Allies**
            
            I'm now focusing on acquiring key city-state allies to bolster our delegate count. With Djenne and Kathmandu captured, I'm assessing our peace status with the remaining Indian allies. Securing Vilnius and potentially flipping others will be crucial to counter India's influence in the World Congress and prevent detrimental votes.
            
            **Securing Key Allies**
            
            I'm now focusing on acquiring key city-state allies to bolster our delegate count. With Djenne and Kathmandu captured, I'm assessing our peace status with the remaining Indian allies. Securing Vilnius and potentially flipping others will be crucial to counter India's influence in the World Congress and prevent detrimental votes.
            
            **Flipping Vilnius Delegate**
            
            I've confirmed that Mombasa is now at war with us due to their alliance with India. My priority has shifted to flipping Vilnius from Celtic influence. By increasing our relationship with Vilnius to Public 100 and Private 100, I aim to secure their delegates and counteract the overwhelming opposition in the World Congress.
            
            **Declaring War on America**
            
            I'm re-evaluating declaring war on America now. While their proximity to our captured territories and combined military strength (with India and the Celts) present a significant threat, I'm considering the implications of their expired peace treaty. This decision will heavily influence our global strategic positioning.
            
            **Analyzing Celtic Assault**
            
            I'm realizing the immediate danger if I declare war on America. The Celts, allied with them, will likely attack our northern and eastern fronts, specifically targeting Rome and Truro. Their concentrated units near these cities, despite being lower tech, pose a significant defensive challenge given our current troop deployments.
            
            **Preparing Atomic Strategy**
            
            I've realized a conventional war against America now would be a costly three-front conflict. It's far more advantageous to focus on securing India's capitals first, then England's, and research Atomic Bombs. This will allow a swift, devastating victory against America and the Celts with nuclear weapons, avoiding prolonged, attritional warfare.
            
            **Planning Atomic Strike**
            
            I've determined that a direct assault on America now would be a costly multi-front war. My revised strategy is to secure India's remaining capitals, then England's, and prioritize researching Atomic Bombs. This will enable a swift, decisive victory using nuclear weapons against America and the Celts, bypassing protracted conflict.
            
            **Planning London's Capture**
            
            I've realized a critical limitation: a 19-turn pact prevents war with the Celts, thus delaying Edinburgh's capture. However, England is currently our enemy. London is coastal and close to our strong forces in Brussels, and our fleet is perfectly positioned to bombard it. This offers an immediate path to capturing an original capital.
            
            **Executing Dual Captures**
            
            My focus is now on simultaneously pushing south to eliminate India and north/west to secure York and London. This strategy leverages our current peace with the Celts and America while maximizing our original capital acquisitions.
            
            **Securing Delhi and London**
            
            I'm now simultaneously pushing to eliminate India by capturing their remaining cities and to secure London, leveraging our fleet and proximity. This dual offensive aims to maximize our original capital acquisitions while our current peace with the Celts and America holds.
            
            **Securing Indian Cities**
            
            I am prioritizing the capture of Indian cities while maintaining maximum hostility with them. I'm also securing London, as England is currently at war with us and the city is close to our forces. My spies are actively gathering intelligence in Indian cities, which is proving beneficial.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomacy Strategy**
            
            I've realized my previous approach to influencing city-states like Vilnius was based on a misunderstanding of the available tools. The `set-relationship` command, I now understand, is strictly for major civilizations, not city-states, forcing a reassessment of how I can gain their delegates and disrupt enemy alliances.
            
            **Setting Flavors for City-States**
            
            I've realized my previous understanding of influencing city-states was incorrect; the `set-relationship` command isn't applicable. Instead, I will focus on setting high `Diplomacy` and `Gold` flavors. This will naturally guide our staff to secure city-state alliances and delegates, especially for Vilnius, by leveraging quest rewards and gold investment.
            
            **Securing London and India**
            
            I'm simultaneously pushing to eliminate India by capturing their remaining cities, especially Thebes, and to secure London, leveraging our fleet and proximity. This dual offensive aims to maximize our original capital acquisitions while our current peace with the Celts and America holds.
            
            **Researching Nuclear Fission**
            
            I've confirmed that researching Nuclear Fission is the most advantageous path forward. This technology will unlock Atomic Bombs, allowing for a decisive and swift victory over America and the Celts, bypassing the need for costly protracted warfare.
            
            **Securing Nuclear Fission**
            
            My focus is now on securing Nuclear Fission research. While Atomic Theory is nearing completion, I'm reinforcing the next step to unlock Atomic Bombs. This technology is crucial for a swift, decisive victory against America and the Celts, avoiding costly conventional warfare and allowing for the eventual capture of Edinburgh and Washington.
            
            **Prioritizing Spaceflight Pioneers**
            
            I'm evaluating the next ideological tenet. Given our current trajectory and pursuit of a Domination victory, prioritizing 'Spaceflight Pioneers' seems most strategic for its unique bonuses to Great Engineers and Scientists, accelerating our endgame capabilities.
            
            **Adopting Free Technologies**
            
            I'm prioritizing the adoption of the "Great Leap Forward" policy. The free technology it provides, combined with the significant spy science boost, will accelerate our research towards nuclear capabilities and ultimately a swift victory over America and the Celts.
            
            **Setting Strategic Flavors**
            
            I am now focused on implementing the specific flavor adjustments for our grand strategy. This involves setting `Conquest` as our primary approach, with heightened emphasis on `Offense`, `Mobilization`, `Ranged`, `Mobile`, `Nuke`, and `Naval` capabilities to prepare for aggressive expansion and eventual nuclear deployment.
            
            **Setting Strategic Flavors**
            
            I am now focused on implementing the specific flavor adjustments for our grand strategy. This involves setting `Conquest` as our primary approach, with heightened emphasis on `Offense`, `Mobilization`, `Ranged`, `Mobile`, `Nuke`, and `Naval` capabilities to prepare for aggressive expansion and eventual nuclear deployment.
            
            **Adjusting Flavors Strategically**
            
            I'm refining our strategic flavors to prioritize Conquest, with heavy emphasis on Offense, Mobilization, Naval, and Nuke capabilities. Diplomacy is now at maximum to secure city-state alliances and block detrimental World Congress votes, while Espionage is set high for tech theft and intelligence gathering.
            
            **Researching Nuclear Fission**
            
            I've confirmed that researching Nuclear Fission is the most advantageous path forward. This technology will unlock Atomic Bombs, allowing for a decisive and swift victory over America and the Celts, bypassing the need for costly protracted warfare.
            
            **Prioritizing Atomic Bomb Development**
            
            I'm prioritizing the research of Nuclear Fission to unlock Atomic Bombs. This strategy will enable a decisive victory against America and the Celts, avoiding the prolonged attrition of a conventional war and paving the way for capturing Edinburgh and Washington.
